// @name         NekoRPG Game Text Localizer
// @namespace    dragonayzer.userscripts
// @version      14.8
// @description  Full English localization: UI, item/enemy/skill names, and all prose (descriptions, dialogue, system messages). See the TOGGLES block at the top of the script to switch layers on/off.
// @match        https://btly0711.github.io/NekoRPG/*
// @match        https://btly0711-github-io.translate.goog/NekoRPG/*
// @run-at       document-start
// @grant        none
// ==/UserScript==

// 9.5: HOT_IDS: rank, clock and reactor readouts are rewritten every frame, so translate them straight off the MutationObserver instead of waiting for the 100ms scan
// 9.6: [基础值: x] is appended into an existing node so its anchored regex was dead; fragment added

// 12.6: maxed skill bars flickered between the compacted and uncompacted renderings on any skill gaining XP every tick (the equipped technique's and active stance's skills): translateHot restored the NAME in the mutation batch but compactMaxedSkillBars only ran on the throttled scan, so it is now called from translateHot too, after the name pass

The act comes from data-bestiary (-100*(zone+1)), which is structural - parsing the 'Zone 4 - 1' cell would break with ENABLE_PROSE off
// 12.9: stance descriptions rendered as '强大的单体Attack秘法.': #stance_list_div lists the stance NAMES before the descriptions that quote them, and applySelector applies every pair in order, so the name pair destroyed the whole-sentence pair and the prose fragment stage chewed the remains. stance_list_div joins the early exact pass; uitest_order now ENFORCES that every shadowed pair sits in a selector an early pass covers (4 across 73 selectors)
// 13.0: skill tooltip joins the no-stutter list while HOVERED: update_displayed_skill_xp_gain rewrites its XP Gain / XP Cost Scaling lines every tick, plus milestone and effect text. Scoped through the game's own reveal selector (.skill_bar_max:hover .skill_tooltip) so it is the one open tooltip rather than all ~111, matched natively with no layout flush - getComputedStyle or offsetParent would visit all of them AND force a flush. annotateXpGain now takes an optional root so the (x1.70e6) suffix is restored in the same batch instead of flickering on its own
// 13.1: game updated V3.40 -> V3.41c (Blood Peak, zone 45): 56 new Chinese strings, 6 already covered compositionally. 19 enemy names -> 02_enemies.tsv (required: it is the sole source of enemy_en_zh, which drives both the name->fragment promotion and bestiaryNames, the reverse map the game's own bestiary hover handler reads back), 反击 spec tag -> 05_ui.tsv as a FORCED FRAG (spliced into combat-log lines), 26 descriptions -> a NEW frozen pair prose/_todo_v341 + done_v341. New pair rather than regenerating any existing _todo: those keys are positional and re-extraction would silently re-pair every English string to a neighbouring Chinese one
// 13.2: BIG_NUMBER_SCIENTIFIC: high-tier gem descriptions get a scientific form appended to their spelled-out numbers (500,000,000 -> 500,000,000 (5.00e8)). The source writes these as 5亿 but the translation expanded them, so formatMyriad had no unit left to convert. Threshold 1e7 so the two 7-digit currency descriptions stay untouched - one already reads '(or written 1MU)' and the other is a unit conversion
// 13.3: gem scientific annotation drops trailing zeroes: 5.00e8 -> 5e8, 1.00e11 -> 1e11. Still rounds to 2 decimals first, so an awkward value caps at 1.23e9 rather than printing every digit
// 13.4: XP Gain annotation drops trailing zeroes too (x1.70e6 -> x1.7e6, x1.00e5 -> x1e5); both annotators now share one sciShort() formatter so their output cannot drift apart
// 13.5: gem scientific annotation moved OUT of the runtime pass and into the translations: annotateBigNumbers, BIG_NUMBER_SCIENTIFIC, BIG_NUMBER_SCI_MIN and BIG_NUM_RE are gone, and 17 rows in items_desc.tsv now carry the annotation in the shipped English. Threshold 1e6, so the mid-tier gems are covered too; the two currency lines are skipped because the number is glued to its unit (1,000,000C) and an annotation would split the token. sciShort() stays - the XP multiplier is computed at runtime and genuinely cannot be baked in
// 13.6: spelled-out magnitudes replaced with sci-notation where a FIGURE is attached: '36-billion defense' -> '3.6e10 defense', '360 billion Agility' -> '3.6e11 Agility'. 22 rows across locations, enemies, dialogue, main and display. Rhetorical uses with no digit are untouched ('a million years old', 'thousands upon thousands'). Exact conversion, no rounding, so 176.4 billion is 1.764e11. Matches what the game itself displays via formatMyriad
// 13.7: reverted three v13.6 conversions that hit flavour prose rather than stats: the 10万~31亿 range (converting one endpoint left 'the sum of 100,000 to 3.1e9', mixed formats in one range) in two dialogue rows, and a direction-giving joke's '8.195 million kilometers'. The rule the pass should have followed: convert a figure the player compares against the UI, leave a figure a character is speaking
// 13.8: gem descriptions keep only the sci form: '500,000,000 (5e8)' -> '5e8'. No precision lost - these are exact powers - and it matches what the game itself renders for the same stat
// 13.9: family roster translated off-throttle so its Chinese frame is never painted: the daily rebuild renders Chinese rows THREE lines tall where the English takes two, so the throttled pass shrank the content under the scrollbar and the view jumped. A MutationObserver callback runs before paint, so this removes the frame rather than restoring the scroll position afterwards. Gated on #family_div's inline display so the ~330 nodes are only walked while the tab is open
// 14.0: family roster scroll drifted one row per daily rebuild even with the Chinese frame gone: the browser's scroll anchoring compensates for the wholesale row replacement but its anchor node no longer exists. #family_div and #family_member_list opt out with overflow-anchor: none
// 14.1: family roster realm names at 10px: they are the longest text in the table and the only cells that wrap, so they alone set the row height
// 14.2: reverted v14.0's overflow-anchor: none. It did not fix the one-row drift, it removed the browser compensation that was hiding a PER-SECOND height change in the family header, and the view then slid to the bottom every second. #family_timer_real is rewritten every tick ('8s' -> '7s'), so the header line changes width and, at English lengths, crosses a wrap boundary
// 14.3: family roster scroll: the two countdown spans are rewritten every tick and change width with their digits, reflowing the header line above the roster inside the same scroller - so the content height changed and a player at the bottom got clamped upward. Fixed-width inline-block boxes on #family_timer_game / #family_timer_real remove the width change at source
// 14.4: rolled back to the v13.9 behaviour and kept only the 10px realm-name change: v14.0's overflow-anchor and v14.3's fixed-width timer spans are both gone, so the roster's scroll behaves as the game delivers it. Both dead ends are recorded in a comment beside the family CSS
// 14.5: family roster: the view crept one row upward on every daily rebuild even after v13.9 removed the Chinese frame, because update_displayed_family_members() recreates every <tr> and so destroys the node the browser had anchored the scroll to - an anchor that no longer exists cannot be compensated for, and the function is not on window so the rebuild cannot be made non-destructive from outside. The offset is now put back instead: a scroll listener caches it (the only free read of scrollTop there is), a rebuild is detected by comparing the header row's node identity against the one we last translated, and the cached value is WRITTEN back in the same pre-paint batch - a write overrides the browser's adjustment for that frame where a read would return it. One forced layout per game day with the tab open, none otherwise; translateHot itself stays free of layout-forcing reads, which uitest_order.js now asserts property by property. Toggles FAMILY_SCROLL_RESTORE and FAMILY_SCROLL_DEBUG; the debug readout logs wanted/found/settled plus scrollHeight, which distinguishes the offset moving from the content moving. NOT overflow-anchor:none (the v14.0 experiment) - anchoring stays on, since it is also absorbing a per-second height change in the header block
// 14.6: housekeeping: the family CSS comment still claimed the roster's scroll was 'left as the game delivers it', which v14.5 made false. Collapsed into a pointer at restoreFamilyScroll(), which is now the authoritative account, keeping only what the two CSS attempts proved: anchoring must stay ON (v14.0 turning it off exposed a per-second header height change it was absorbing), and v14.3's fixed-width timer spans were an attempt to make that removal survivable rather than a fix in their own right. No code change
// 14.7: trader gains a 5th category button, Materials: the game's Misc bucket with the Parts hidden. Misc is .trader_item_other/_loot/_material/_component/_book all on --trader_other_display, so materials and Parts could only be filtered together - and Parts are the tall expensive rows you scroll past while stocking up. Same shape as the Zones tab: no row is copied or moved, the button sets the game's own three display variables exactly as showOnlyTraderOther() does and one CSS rule (#trader_inventory_div.tl_materials_only .trader_item_component) hides the Parts, so buy buttons, tooltips, sorting and the click-to-buy handler stay the game's own. The class sits on the container, which survives update_displayed_trader_inventory's textContent='' rebuild. Row re-budgeted to 62/72/86/72/98 + margins = the 400px panel exactly, since the game's own 4x98px already fills it and a fifth would wrap. Toggle TRADER_MATERIALS_FILTER; covered by the new uitest_tradefilter.js, which asserts the rule hides Parts and ONLY Parts
// 14.8: trader category button renamed Mats and the row levelled to five equal 78px buttons (78 + 1px margin either side = 80, x5 = the 400px panel exactly). New Add all button, first in the Trade / Cancel row, which puts every row currently ON SCREEN into the buy list - displayed rather than 'all materials' on purpose, so paired with the category buttons it means whatever you are looking at and the two features stay independent (Mats + Add all = a one-click crafting restock). It adds nothing to the trade logic: every row already has an 'all' button and #trader_inventory_div carries the game's delegated handler, so this is those buttons pressed for you, through the same add_to_buying_list() that clamps Infinity to the trader's stock. Still only fills the cart; buying is still acceptTrade(). Two things the loop must get right and uitest_tradefilter.js now models both: the list is re-queried after EVERY click because the game rebuilds it with textContent='' and a cached node is detached and fails silently, and the row selector carries :not(.item_to_trade) because the trader panel also shows the player's own staged sales, which would be un-sold instead of bought. offsetParent is the visibility test - it forces a layout, which is acceptable in a click handler and nowhere else. Row re-budgeted to 3x131 + margins = 399 of 400. Toggle TRADER_ADD_ALL; no affordability check, matching what clicking each row by hand would do

(function () {
    'use strict';

    // ---- General TODO ---------------------------------------------------
    // - 万-suffixed numbers (e.g. "276.47万" = 2,764,700) are currently
    //   left as-is everywhere. Converting these to scientific notation
    //   (or plain comma-grouped numbers) for English readability is a
    //   separate task from text translation and hasn't been started.
    // -----------------------------------------------------------------

    // ---- Style overrides (not text translation) ----------------------
    // Plain CSS fixes for spacing/layout issues, injected once. Unlike
    // everything else in this script, these don't need rescanning —
    // a CSS rule applies automatically to every matching element,
    // including ones added later. !important is used since it's not
    // known whether the original value comes from an inline style or
    // the game's own stylesheet, and this guarantees the override wins
    // either way.
    const styleOverrides = document.createElement('style');
    styleOverrides.textContent = `
        #trader_cost_mult_value { padding-top: 0px !important; }

        /* --- Deepfrost engine: sized for Chinese, retuned for English ----
           Chinese is ~2 characters where English needs a word, so several
           absolutely-positioned controls overlapped once translated. */

        /* Requested: 13px across this screen. This alone fixes the column
           collision - the longest left-column line ("Porous Ice Crystal : 116
           pts / 0.1646 m [+10] [+100] [+ALL][-ALL]") comes in around 410px,
           inside the 448px the game gives it, so #engine_result_stats stays
           where the game puts it and no gap opens up. */
        #engine_div { font-size: 13px !important; }

        /* The piston buttons are absolutely positioned at 40px - enough for
           压缩, not for "Compress" (~52px at 13px bold). Widen and re-space
           inside the 264px #piston_buttons strip; 192 + 60 = 252px. */
        #piston_compress, #piston_expand, #piston_fill, #piston_defill {
            width: 60px !important;
        }
        #piston_expand { left: 64px !important; }
        #piston_fill   { left: 128px !important; }
        #piston_defill { left: 192px !important; }

        /* "Move Piston" wrapped to two lines inside 90px (the icon eats ~18px).
           Ends at 390px, clear of #leave_engine at 400px. */
        #piston_change { width: 110px !important; }

        /* --- Zones tab: hide everything that is not a zone row -----------
           Only bites while #bestiary_list carries .tl_zones_only, which only
           the Zones tab adds, so this rule is inert the rest of the time and
           costs nothing when ZONES_TAB is off. .tl_zone_row is set by the same
           pass that colours the zone links. */
        #bestiary_list.tl_zones_only .bestiary_entry_div:not(.tl_zone_row) {
            display: none;
        }

        /* --- Materials category: Misc, minus the Parts -------------------
           The game files both under one variable:
               .trader_item_other, _loot, _material, _component, _book
                   { display: var(--trader_other_display); }
           so its Misc button cannot show materials without Parts. This rule
           hides the Parts, and only while #trader_inventory_div carries
           .tl_materials_only - which only the Materials button adds, so it is
           inert otherwise and costs nothing with the toggle off.
           No !important: an id plus a class outranks the game's class-only
           rule on its own, and staying at plain specificity leaves the game
           free to hide these rows for its own reasons. */
        #trader_inventory_div.tl_materials_only .trader_item_component {
            display: none;
        }

        /* --- "Add all", third button in the Trade / Cancel row -----------
           Trade and Cancel are styled by id and share their rule with
           .trader_sorting_button, so there is no class to borrow and widening
           that selector would resize the sorting buttons too - the look is
           repeated here instead. Width is NOT set here: addTradeAllButton()
           sets all three inline, so with the toggle off the game's own
           2x197px row is untouched. Inert while the element does not exist.

           No background of its own. Trade is green and Cancel is red, and a
           third colour would be a claim about what this button means; plain
           outline says "does something, commits nothing", which is accurate -
           it only fills the cart. The hover borrows the game's own
           --active_button_color, so it follows whichever theme is active. */
        #tl_trade_add_all {
            float: left;
            text-align: center;
            outline: 1px solid white;
            margin: -1px 1px;
            cursor: pointer;
            font-size: 16px;
        }
        #tl_trade_add_all:hover {
            background-color: var(--active_button_color);
        }

        /* NB: the bestiary loot-name fix is NOT a rule here. It has to apply
           only to names long enough to wrap, and CSS cannot select on text
           length - see fitLootNames() in the generated block. */

        /* --- Family roster: sized for Chinese, retuned for English -------
           #skills_and_stances_div is 400px and cannot grow: #journal_div sits
           at left:1230px, only 10px past its right edge. #family_div already
           reserves a vertical scrollbar (overflow-y: scroll), so the table has
           roughly 385px to live in.

           The game's own columns are 60/75/75/75/75 at the 16px body default,
           which fits the 4-5 glyphs each Chinese header needs. "Breakthroughs"
           and "Skyhigh-Tier Rank 8" are ~3x wider, and a <select> sizes itself
           to its widest OPTION - so untouched, the translated table pushed a
           horizontal scrollbar into the panel.

           12px throughout, and the realm column is deliberately narrow: realm
           names wrap cleanly at their spaces ("All-Things-Tier" / "Peak"), so
           width spent there buys nothing, while the strategy <select> cannot
           wrap at all and needs every pixel. */
        /* The roster's scroll is fixed in JS, in restoreFamilyScroll() - see
           the note there for why. Two CSS attempts at it are NOT to be
           retried, both because they fought the browser's scroll anchoring
           instead of the actual cause:

           overflow-anchor:none on #family_div (v14.0) turned anchoring off and
           the view then slid to the bottom every second instead of drifting
           one row per rebuild. Anchoring is absorbing a per-second height
           change in the header block above the table, and must stay on.

           Fixed-width boxes on #family_timer_game / #family_timer_real (v14.3)
           tried to remove that per-second change at its source so the first
           one could be kept. Neither is needed: anchoring already handles the
           header, and the rebuild is handled where it happens. */

        #family_member_list { font-size: 12px !important; }
        /* Realm names are the longest text in the table ("All-Things-Tier
           Basic", "Dust-Tier Intermediate") and the only cells that wrap, so
           they alone set the row height. 10px rather than the table's 12px
           lets more of them sit on one line. Scoped to this column: the
           numbers and the strategy select stay legible at 12px/11px. */
        #family_member_list .member_list_realm  {
            width: 84px !important;
            font-size: 10px !important;
        }
        #family_member_list .member_list_num    { width: 46px !important; }
        #family_member_list .member_list_change { width: 40px !important; }
        #family_member_list .member_list_alti   { width: 104px !important; }
        /* The select carries the longest strings in the table ("[1] Regular
           Work"), so it gets its own smaller size rather than shrinking every
           number alongside it. */
        #family_member_list .family_ali {
            width: 102px !important;
            font-size: 11px !important;
        }
    `;
    (document.head || document.documentElement).appendChild(styleOverrides);
    // -------------------------------------------------------------------

    // ==== TOGGLES =====================================================
    // Every switch in the script, in one place. All are plain consts read at
    // runtime, so flipping one and reloading is enough - nothing else needs
    // touching. Declared here, ahead of everything that uses them, including
    // the generated block further down.
    //
    // Two groups, and the split matters. TRANSLATION switches change what the
    // text SAYS; VISUAL OVERRIDES change what the game LOOKS like, and none of
    // them are needed to read the game in English. If a redesign is unwelcome,
    // the visual group is the part to turn off.
    //
    // NOT in either group: the `styleOverrides` stylesheet above. That is not
    // polish - English is roughly three times wider than the Chinese those
    // panels were laid out for, so without it controls overlap and tables push
    // past their panel. It stays on with the translation it exists to serve.

    // ---- TRANSLATION -------------------------------------------------

    // Item-name translation (a separate subsystem from the prose layer).
    // Flip off to drop it instantly if it ever costs too much FPS.
    const ENABLE_ITEM_NAMES = true;

    // The prose layer: exact matches, template regexes and fragments.
    // This is the bulk of the translation.
    const ENABLE_PROSE = true;

    // Myriad-unit numbers -> exponential notation (972万 -> 9.72e6).
    const ENABLE_NUMBER_FORMAT = true;

    // ---- VISUAL OVERRIDES --------------------------------------------
    // Master switch for everything below: false means the script only
    // translates and the game is left looking exactly as it shipped.
    // The individual switches are then irrelevant - this one wins.
    const ENABLE_VISUAL_OVERRIDES = true;

    // Compact a maxed skill bar by stripping " : level N/N" off the name,
    // folding N/N into the "Max!" slot, and joining the name with non-breaking
    // spaces so it cannot wrap.
    const COMPACT_MAXED_SKILL_BARS = true;

    // Recolour a maxed bar: flat dark block, lighter outline, progress strip
    // collapsed to 0px. A maxed bar is always 100% full, so the strip carries
    // no information and only adds visual noise.
    const MAXED_BAR_HIGHLIGHT = true;
    // .skill_bar_text is WHITE, so the fill has to stay dark enough to read it.
    // #14532d gives ~9.7:1 against white, and the lighter #008000 border keeps
    // the bar outlined so it still reads as "green = maxed" down a long list.
    // Never use the game's own accent rgb(3,255,36) as a FILL - white on it is
    // ~1.4:1, i.e. effectively invisible. It is fine as a border.
    const MAXED_BAR_BG = '#14532d';
    const MAXED_BAR_BORDER = '#008000';

    // Flip the icon on "Return"/"Quick Return" travel options so the arrow
    // points back the way you came. The game gives every travel option the
    // same right-pointing glyph, and nothing in the markup distinguishes a
    // return from a forward move - both are .travel_normal - so this is
    // decided from the translated label.
    const MIRROR_RETURN_ICONS = true;

    // Replace the game's 'directions' signpost on travel options with a plain
    // arrow. Independent of MIRROR_RETURN_ICONS, though they were made for
    // each other: a mirrored signpost is still just a signpost, while a
    // mirrored arrow genuinely points left.
    const REPLACE_TRAVEL_ICON = true;
    // Other sensible Material Icons ligatures: 'arrow_forward', 'east',
    // 'arrow_right_alt', 'double_arrow'.
    // Two glyphs are deliberately left alone: the combat options'
    // 'warning_amber' (a hazard marker, not a direction) and the signpost on
    // JUMPS - Fast Travel and Quick Return - which teleport you to a remembered
    // place rather than stepping through the map. Jumps are never mirrored
    // either: they are neither forward nor back.
    const TRAVEL_ICON = 'forward';

    // Join the current location's name with non-breaking spaces so it never
    // wraps inside the 250px #location_name_div - the whole name, separators
    // included. A name too wide for the box overflows rather than wrapping;
    // nothing resizes or clips it.
    const NBSP_LOCATION_NAMES = true;

    // Colour the bestiary's clickable ZONE rows (the 【…】 links that fast-travel
    // you there, plus their act numbers). They are buried among hundreds of
    // enemy rows they otherwise look identical to.
    const HIGHLIGHT_BESTIARY_LINKS = true;
    // One colour per ACT, in order 1-6. The game has exactly six realm colour
    // classes and exactly six acts, so acts 2-6 reuse the realm colour the act
    // corresponds to - Earth green, Sky blue, Skyhigh purple, Domain pink,
    // World yellow. Act 1's realm colour is white, which is what every enemy
    // row already uses, so it gets a colour of its own instead: a muted dust
    // tan, thematic for 微尘级 / Dust-Tier and sitting visually BELOW the
    // saturated later acts. It also fills the one large empty gap in the hue
    // wheel (~35°, between the pink at 320° and the yellow at 57°), so no two
    // acts can be confused.
    const BESTIARY_ACT_COLORS = [
        '#C8A165',      // Act 1  dust tan      (realm_basic is white - replaced)
        '#75E97E',      // Act 2  realm_terra   Earth
        '#6FAEE4',      // Act 3  realm_sky     Sky
        '#C76EE7',      // Act 4  realm_cloudy  Skyhigh
        '#E06BBB',      // Act 5  realm_domain  Domain
        '#F0EA28',      // Act 6  realm_world   World
    ];
    // Fallback only, for a zone row whose act cannot be read off data-bestiary.
    // Nothing in the game should hit it - every zone row carries that attribute
    // - so seeing yellowgreen in the list means the act derivation has broken.
    const BESTIARY_LINK_COLOR = '#9acd32';

    // Append a scientific form to the skill tooltip's XP multiplier:
    //     XP Gain: x1704855.1  ->  XP Gain: x1704855.1 (x1.70e6)
    // Appended, not substituted - the exact figure still matters when comparing
    // two skills.
    const XP_GAIN_SCIENTIFIC = true;
    // Only annotate from here up. Below it the scientific form is LONGER than
    // the number it explains ("x1.6" -> "x1.60e0"), so it would be noise.
    // 1e5 = the point where the plain decimal runs past six digits; lower this
    // to annotate more aggressively, raise it to annotate less.
    const XP_GAIN_SCI_MIN = 1e5;

    // Put the part tier at the front of the name in the crafting component
    // list, where you pick by tier but the tier only shows in the tooltip:
    //     Skybreaker Wheel Hub  ->  T16 · Skybreaker Wheel Hub
    // Plain materials have no tier and are left alone.
    const CRAFTING_TIER_PREFIX = true;

    // Add a 5th journal tab, "Zones": the bestiary filtered down to just its
    // clickable zone rows, i.e. a slim teleport menu instead of scrolling ~560
    // entries. Nothing is duplicated - the same list is shown with the enemy
    // rows hidden - so tooltips and teleport clicks are the game's own, and
    // only zones you have already discovered can appear.
    // Off means the journal keeps its original four tabs, untouched.
    const ZONES_TAB = true;

    // Add a 5th trader category button, "Materials": the game's Misc bucket
    // with the Parts hidden. Misc is .trader_item_other/_loot/_material/
    // _component/_book all on one display variable, so crafting materials and
    // Parts can only be filtered together - and Parts are the bulky, expensive
    // rows you are scrolling PAST when you are stocking up on materials.
    // Nothing is copied or moved: the button sets the game's own display
    // variables exactly as its Misc button does and hides the Parts with one
    // CSS rule, so the rows stay the game's own and their buy buttons,
    // tooltips and sorting are untouched.
    // Off means the trader keeps its original four categories.
    const TRADER_MATERIALS_FILTER = true;

    // Add an "Add all" button beside Trade / Cancel, which puts every row
    // currently ON SCREEN into the buy list. Displayed rather than "all
    // materials" on purpose: paired with the category buttons above it means
    // whatever you are looking at, so Mats + Add all is a one-click crafting
    // restock without the button having to know what a material is.
    // It only fills the cart - buying is still the Trade button - and the
    // count is the game's own "all", clamped to what the trader holds.
    // NB: nothing checks affordability. Adding more than you can pay for is
    // allowed and leaves Trade greyed out, exactly as it would if you had
    // clicked each row's "all" by hand; click rows to remove them again.
    // Off means the trade panel keeps its original two buttons.
    const TRADER_ADD_ALL = true;

    // The family roster rebuilds once per game day, and the view creeps one
    // row upward every time it does. Put the scroll offset back afterwards.
    // Off means the roster scrolls exactly as the game leaves it.
    const FAMILY_SCROLL_RESTORE = true;
    // Log what the restore actually saw, once per rebuild:
    //     [NekoRPG TL] family rebuild: wanted 812, found 780, settled at 812
    //                  (scrollHeight 1904 -> 1904)
    // Diagnostic only, and off by default. If the drift ever comes back this
    // says whether the offset moved (wanted != found) or the CONTENT did
    // (scrollHeight changed) - two different problems with two different
    // fixes, and not distinguishable by watching the panel.
    const FAMILY_SCROLL_DEBUG = false;
    // ==================================================================

    // substring search) — with 400+ entries, a shorter name being
    // contained inside a longer, unrelated one becomes likely (this is
    // the 剑/巨剑徽章 bug), so this list intentionally does not use the
    // same matching approach as the rest of the script.

        '[出云落月[领域五重]': '[Cloudrise, Moonfall [Domain Tier 5]',

    // directOnly=true checks only el's direct text-node children
    // (e.g. preview text sitting right before a nested tooltip),
    // rather than walking the full subtree — avoids redundantly
    // re-processing a nested .recipe_tooltip that's already handled
    // by its own call.
    function textNodesOf(el, directOnly) {
        if (directOnly) {
            return Array.from(el.childNodes).filter((n) => n.nodeType === 3);
        }
        const out = [];
        const walker = document.createTreeWalker(el, NodeFilter.SHOW_TEXT);
        let n;
        while ((n = walker.nextNode())) out.push(n);
        return out;
    }

    function applyItemNames(selector, directOnly) {
        if (!ENABLE_ITEM_NAMES) return;
        for (const el of document.querySelectorAll(selector)) {
            for (const node of textNodesOf(el, directOnly)) {
                const original = node.textContent;
                const trimmed = original.trim();

                // Whole node is exactly an item name.
                if (itemNames[trimmed]) {
                    node.textContent = original.replace(trimmed, itemNames[trimmed]);
                    el.classList.add('translated');
                    continue;
                }
                // "ItemName, 140%, x1" (selection previews) — exact
                // text before the first comma.
                const commaIdx = trimmed.indexOf(',');
                if (commaIdx > -1) {
                    const candidate = trimmed.slice(0, commaIdx).trim();
                    if (itemNames[candidate]) {
                        node.textContent = original.replace(candidate, itemNames[candidate]);
                        el.classList.add('translated');
                        continue;
                    }
                }
                // "ItemName x0/4" (materials lists) — exact text before
                // a space-then-"x"-then-digit boundary.
                const spaceX = trimmed.match(/^(.+?)\s+x\d/);
                if (spaceX && itemNames[spaceX[1]]) {
                    node.textContent = original.replace(spaceX[1], itemNames[spaceX[1]]);
                    el.classList.add('translated');
                    continue;
                }
                // "[ItemName]+[ItemName]+..." (assembled-equipment
                // component list) — any number of bracketed segments;
                // each one is checked independently, so a bracket that
                // isn't a recognized item name is left as-is rather
                // than skipping the whole line.
                if (/\[[^[\]]+\]/.test(trimmed)) {
                    const swapped = original.replace(/\[([^[\]]+)\]/g, (whole, inner) => {
                        const t = itemNames[inner.trim()];
                        return t ? `[${t}]` : whole;
                    });
                    if (swapped !== original) {
                        node.textContent = swapped;
                        el.classList.add('translated');
                    }
                }
                // '"ItemName" x1, "ItemName" x1' (message/event log
                // entries) — same idea as the bracket pattern above,
                // just quote-delimited instead.
                if (/"[^"]+"/.test(trimmed)) {
                    const swapped2 = node.textContent.replace(/"([^"]+)"/g, (whole, inner) => {
                        const t = itemNames[inner.trim()];
                        return t ? `"${t}"` : whole;
                    });
                    if (swapped2 !== node.textContent) {
                        node.textContent = swapped2;
                        el.classList.add('translated');
                    }
                }
                // "使用 ItemName , ..." (single gem-use messages) — the
                // item name sits between spaces before a comma,
                // unlike the other patterns where the name starts at
                // the very beginning of the text. \s* (not \s) since
                // a same-pass buttons-mechanism cleanup pair may have
                // already tightened " , " to ", " by the time this
                // runs. Matches any non-whitespace run before the
                // comma, not just after a known prefix — safe
                // regardless, since it still has to be an exact
                // dictionary hit to do anything.
                const spaceComma = node.textContent.match(/(\S+)\s*,/);
                if (spaceComma && itemNames[spaceComma[1]]) {
                    node.textContent = node.textContent.replace(spaceComma[1], itemNames[spaceComma[1]]);
                    el.classList.add('translated');
                }
                // "..., ItemName." (bulk-use messages) — reverse of the
                // pattern above, item name follows the comma instead
                // of preceding it, ending at a period. Verified this
                // doesn't misfire on the single-gem message's longer
                // ", Stat increased by N[Soft Cap]." tail, since \S+
                // can't cross the space inside that phrase to reach a
                // period — same safety net either way (exact
                // dictionary hit required).
                const commaThenItem = node.textContent.match(/,\s*(\S+)\./);
                if (commaThenItem && itemNames[commaThenItem[1]]) {
                    node.textContent = node.textContent.replace(commaThenItem[1], itemNames[commaThenItem[1]]);
                    el.classList.add('translated');
                }
                // "SkillName : level X/Y" (skill list bar labels) —
                // skill names reuse itemNames rather than a separate
                // dictionary, since they're just as discrete/unique as
                // item names and an exact-match hit is exact-match
                // regardless of which conceptual bucket the string
                // came from.
                const skillLevel = node.textContent.match(/^(.+?)\s*:\s*level/);
                if (skillLevel && itemNames[skillLevel[1]]) {
                    node.textContent = node.textContent.replace(skillLevel[1], itemNames[skillLevel[1]]);
                    el.classList.add('translated');
                }
            }
        }
    }
    // Wired up in scan() below: recipe tooltips, recipe_name headers,
    // and selection-option preview text.
    // -------------------------------------------------------------------

    // Shared component names, reused by both the recipe headers and the
    // "select a component" dropdown prompts so they always agree.
    // Longer/more specific terms are listed first — several longer
    // terms here contain a shorter term elsewhere in the file
    // (e.g. 剑刃/剑柄 contain 剑), and matching runs top to bottom
    // without stopping, so the longer one has to fire first or the
    // shorter one would partially clobber it.
    const componentPairs = [
        ['剑刃', 'Sword Blade'],
        ['剑柄', 'Sword Hilt'],
        ['三叉戟头', 'Trident Head'],
        ['头部内甲', 'Head (Inner)'],
        ['头部外甲', 'Head (Outer)'],
        ['胸部内甲', 'Chest (Inner)'],
        ['胸部外甲', 'Chest (Outer)'],
        ['腿部内甲', 'Legs (Inner)'],
        ['腿部外甲', 'Legs (Outer)'],
        ['脚部内甲', 'Feet (Inner)'],
        ['脚部外甲', 'Feet (Outer)'],
    ];

    // CSS selector -> list of [original Chinese, replacement] pairs to
    // look for within each matched element's subtree. Use "#some_id" for
    // a single element, or ".some_class" when several elements share a
    // class but have no id of their own. Every matching pair in a list
    // gets applied to a given text node (not just the first), so a
    // template phrase and an embedded term can both translate at once.
    //
    // Short/standalone recipe names (single words with no other
    // qualifying text) are scoped via [data-recipe_id="..."] rather
    // than matched as free text — the game's crafting item pool is
    // large enough that a short term like 剑 ("sword") will legitimately
    // appear inside unrelated item names (巨剑徽章, "Giant Sword Emblem").
    // data-recipe_id is set to the exact recipe name, so it's an exact
    // match rather than a "does this text contain X" search.
    // Stat names — includes both the short forms used on equipped
    // items/character stats (攻击/防御/攻速) and the longer, more formal
    // forms used specifically on crafting components (攻击力/攻击速度/
    // 暴击概率/防御力), which are different strings, not abbreviations
    // of each other. Long forms listed first since several contain a
    // short form as a substring (攻击力 contains 攻击, 防御力 contains 防御).
    const statPairs = [
        ['生命恢复[%]', 'Health Regeneration [%]'],
        ['生命恢复', 'Health Regeneration'],
        ['生命上限', 'Health Cap'],
        ['生命', 'Health'],
        ['基础属性:', 'Base Attributes:'],
        ['预期属性:', 'Expected Attributes:'],
        ['部件等级:', 'Part Tier:'],
        ['品质:', 'Quality:'],
        ['类型: 剑', 'Type: Sword'],
        ['类型: 三叉戟', 'Type: Trident'],
        ['防御力', 'Defense Power'],
        ['攻击力', 'Attack Power'],
        ['攻击速度', 'Attack Speed'],
        ['暴击概率', 'Crit Probability'],
        ['暴击伤害', 'Crit Damage'],
        ['暴击率', 'Crit Rate'],
        ['防御', 'Defense'],
        ['攻击', 'Attack'],
        ['攻速', 'Attack Speed'],
        ['敏捷', 'Agility'],
        ['暴率', 'Crit Rate'],
        ['普攻倍率', 'Basic Attack Multiplier'],
        ['爆伤', 'Crit Damage'],

    // Travel labels that walk BACKWARD, so the arrow is mirrored. Generated
    // from locations.js leave_text (backward by construction) plus a curated
    // list of connected_location custom_text. NOT a prefix rule: 'Leave the
    // village' and 'Leave the safe path' both go forward.
    const BACKWARD_LABELS = new Set([
        'Back off',
        'Climb out',
        'Flee quickly',
        'Get away',
        'Give up',

        'Leave the core area for now',
        'Leave the final battle ground',
